# Register additions: task research-release-data

Branch `data/release-measurements`. These entries are the register-format rendering of the rows added to `data/experimental/release_measurements.csv` (E-157 to E-424 and E-425 to E-540; the earlier rows E-316 to E-348 and E-349 to E-373 were withdrawn and replaced, see Tables 31e and 31f) and `data/temperature/temperature_histories.csv` (TH-123 to TH-157). The CSV files are the machine-readable source; the row identifiers are identical. `data/reference/STRATO_Parameter_Register.md` and `B6_audit_log.md` are not edited by this task. All existing rows, evidence classes, statuses and citations are unchanged. The evidence-class and status vocabulary of the register (Section 0.2) is used without additions. The new values are not validation of any model: they are measurements to compare with, with the limits stated in each row and in `stage-b/btcs/research-release-data.md`.

Vocabulary note: some descriptive labels not present in the existing CSV columns are used and are proposed for the register: `program` "AGR-2"; `fuel_type` "Variant 2" (AGR-1 capsule 2 fuel) and "UCO TRISO" (AGR-2); `group` "loose particles" (4-4-2 loose-particle test); `time_basis` "elapsed time from start of heating" and "end of irradiation (mass balance)". No new evidence class, status or column is introduced.

### Table 31a. AGR-1 total release at the end of each isothermal safety test (Sr-90, Ag-110m, Cs-134, Eu-154, Kr-85)

Tabulated values (Stempien et al. 2023, Table 14, printed p. 25). Compact fraction = released activity divided by the calculated compact inventory at end of irradiation plus one day; one particle inventory is 2.4e-4 of a compact. These are tabulated counterparts of the digitised totals in E-015 to E-026, E-046 to E-059, E-063 to E-078, E-080 to E-087 and E-090 to E-095.

| No. | Variable | Symbol | Value | Unit | Material / Nuclide | Temperature range | Uncertainty / data class / time | PARFUME, same source (calculated) | Note | Applicability to one intact particle | Source / APA citation | Location | Link | Evidence class | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E-157 | total compact release fraction | $f_{\mathrm{rel}}$ | $3\times10^{-5}$ | 1 | Sr-90; 3-2-2 (Baseline) | 1600 °C | M printed table; t = n/s h (end of test) |  | printed as 3.00E-5 (compact fraction) = 1.24E-1 particle equivalents (source column); denominator: calculated compact inventory decay-corrected to end of irradiation plus one day (p. 10); no per-value uncertainty stated (p. 10 gives a general 5 % systematic gamma error plus counting statistics); SiC failures 0; TRISO failures 0 (same table) | compact with intact particles only; includes in-pile inventory already in the matrix (Stempien et al. (2023) printed p. 27 and pp. 31-32 state that part of the Ag-110m, most of the Eu-154 and, in hotter-irradiated compacts, most of the Sr-90 released in safety tests was inventory released in-pile and held in OPyC and matrix, while release through intact coatings during the test is also stated for Ag-110m at 1800 C and for Sr-90 at 1800 C in AGR-2 compacts with small matrix inventories); the table does not separate the two contributions, so the total is not attributable to diffusion through a single intact particle during the test | Stempien et al. ### Table 31c. AGR-1 capsule mass balance, primary source (in-pile release from the compacts)

Demkowicz et al. (2013), INL/EXT-13-28483, Table 7, printed p. 20. The Sr-90 capsule totals exclude the graphite spacers (only metallic components and graphite holders are included); values printed without < may include minimum-detectable-activity contributions of up to 50 %.

### Table 31d. AGR-1 capsule mass balance, summary table of 2023

Stempien et al. (2023) Table 11, printed p. 18. Differs from Table 31c by up to about 8 % in some entries; both are kept. For capsule 4 Eu-154 the primary source gives the upper bound <1.4E-4 (E-289) while this table prints 1.30E-4 (E-313) without <; E-289 is recommended for comparisons. The same Sr-90 and minimum-detectable-activity limits apply as in Table 31c.

### Table 31e. AGR-1 time-dependent cumulative release (digitised from Stempien et al. 2023, Figs. 9 and 12)

Four Ag-110m series and two Sr-90 series that can be identified individually (by end value in Table 14 and hold duration). The figures are vector drawings, so the points are the plotted markers (vertices of the plotted lines), recorded at their own times with the reading uncertainty stated in each row, as a subset of the markers where the note says so. These rows replace the earlier rows E-316 to E-348, several of which had been read off the connecting line at times where no marker is plotted (withdrawn). The other curves of these figures are not digitised.

| No. | Variable | Symbol | Value | Unit | Material / Nuclide | Temperature range | Uncertainty / data class / time | PARFUME, same source (calculated) | Note | Applicability to one intact particle | Source / APA citation | Location | Link | Evidence class | Status |

### Table 31f. AGR-1 compact 4-4-2 loose particles at 1800 C (digitised from ORNL/LTR-2014/486 Fig. 4)

Cumulative release in particle equivalents of 75 particles without matrix. Includes five particles with failed TRISO. All 16 plotted markers of each series are recorded at their own times; these rows replace the earlier rows E-349 to E-373, which were interpolated between markers (withdrawn). The source's own conclusion is that the Ag and Eu release came mostly through intact SiC (printed p. 5, quoted in the rows). Eu-155 is in the legend of the figure and is plotted as a line of 13 vertices from about 47 h without markers; it is not recorded.

| No. | Variable | Symbol | Value | Unit | Material / Nuclide | Temperature range | Uncertainty / data class / time | PARFUME, same source (calculated) | Note | Applicability to one intact particle | Source / APA citation | Location | Link | Evidence class | Status |

| E-493 | cumulative release in particle equivalents (75 loose particles) | $f_{\mathrm{rel}}$ | ~ $3.78\times10^{-2}$ | particles | Sr-90; 4-4-2 loose particles (75 particles) (Variant 3) | 1800 °C | R-dig; t = 23.4 h (elapsed time from start of heating) |  | digitised from the vector content of the figure: each plotted marker is a 6 pt image whose position is stored in the PDF page; marker centres read with the axes calibrated on the axis lines (0 to 700 h, 0 to 40 particle equivalents); reading uncertainty +/-0.05 particle equivalents (0.03 from the stored marker resolution, plus the rounding to three figures of values above 10 and a centre offset of about 0.04 at the zero line, found by the independent verification) and +/-0.3 h in time (the stored positions have a 0.12 pt resolution); the 16 plotted markers of the series are all recorded, the earlier rows between the markers (E-349 to E-373) were interpolated and are withdrawn; the marker times are probably the ends of the deposition cup collection periods (stated for the cups of the companion report ORNL/LTR-2014/485, not stated for this figure); unit is the average particle inventory (INL/ECAR-958 Rev. 2) summed over the sample, not a fraction per particle; time-dependent data were scaled by the average cup collection efficiency; Table 1 (printed p. 4) lists 44.7 % of the detected Sr-90 on the deposition cups, 52.5 % on tantalum parts and 2.8 % on the graphite holder, so the cup-based time dependence is scaled by a cup efficiency of about 45 %; five particles had TRISO failure (three during heat-up and two at 1800 C); the Fig. 4 legend also lists Eu-155, which is plotted as a line of 13 vertices from about 47 h without markers and is not recorded; heating history in TH rows AGR1-LP-4-4-2 (value at the zero line of the axis: not distinguishable from zero within the reading uncertainty) | 75 loose particles deconsolidated from compact 4-4-2, no matrix, individually gamma scanned, tested at 1800 C for about 650 h; nearest AGR-1 setting to a single particle, but five particles had failed TRISO and no end-of-irradiation profile is given; the source conclusion on transport through intact SiC (printed p. 5) names silver and europium only, not strontium | Hunn et al. t = 35.9 h (elapsed time from start of heating) |  | digitised from the vector content of the figure: each plotted marker is a 6 pt image whose position is stored in the PDF page; marker centres read with the axes calibrated on the axis lines (0 to 700 h, 0 to 40 particle equivalents); reading uncertainty +/-0.05 particle equivalents (0.03 from the stored marker resolution, plus the rounding to three figures of values above 10 and a centre offset of about 0.04 at the zero line, found by the independent verification) and +/-0.3 h in time (the stored positions have a 0.12 pt resolution); the 16 plotted markers of the series are all recorded, the earlier rows between the markers (E-349 to E-373) were interpolated and are withdrawn; the marker times are probably the ends of the deposition cup collection periods (stated for the cups of the companion report ORNL/LTR-2014/485, not stated for this figure); unit is the average particle inventory (INL/ECAR-958 Rev. 2) summed over the sample, not a fraction per particle; time-dependent data were scaled by the average cup collection efficiency; Table 1 (printed p. 4) lists 44.7 % of the detected Sr-90 on the deposition cups, 52.5 % on tantalum parts and 2.8 % on the graphite holder, so the cup-based time dependence is scaled by a cup efficiency of about 45 %; five particles had TRISO failure (three during heat-up and two at 1800 C); the Fig. 4 legend also lists Eu-155, which is plotted as a line of 13 vertices from about 47 h without markers and is not recorded; t = 628.1 h (elapsed time from start of heating) |  | digitised from the vector content of the figure: each plotted marker is a 6 pt image whose position is stored in the PDF page; marker centres read with the axes calibrated on the axis lines (0 to 700 h, 0 to 40 particle equivalents); reading uncertainty +/-0.05 particle equivalents (0.03 from the stored marker resolution, plus the rounding to three figures of values above 10 and a centre offset of about 0.04 at the zero line, found by the independent verification) and +/-0.3 h in time (the stored positions have a 0.12 pt resolution); the 16 plotted markers of the series are all recorded, the earlier rows between the markers (E-349 to E-373) were interpolated and are withdrawn; the marker times are probably the ends of the deposition cup collection periods (stated for the cups of the companion report ORNL/LTR-2014/485, not stated for this figure); unit is the average particle inventory (INL/ECAR-958 Rev. 2) summed over the sample, not a fraction per particle; time-dependent data were scaled by the average cup collection efficiency; Table 1 (printed p. 4) lists 44.7 % of the detected Sr-90 on the deposition cups, 52.5 % on tantalum parts and 2.8 % on the graphite holder, so the cup-based time dependence is scaled by a cup efficiency of about 45 %; five particles had TRISO failure (three during heat-up and two at 1800 C); the Fig. 4 legend also lists Eu-155, which is plotted as a line of 13 vertices from about 47 h without markers and is not recorded; ### Table 31g. AGR-1 compact 4-2-2 multi-temperature test, Ag-110m collected on deposition cups (digitised from ORNL/LTR-2014/485 Fig. 1)

Fraction of the calculated compact inventory collected on the cups; about 60 % of the Ag-110m inventory had already left the compact during irradiation.

### Table 31h. AGR-2 UCO safety tests, total release at the end of each test (Sr-90 and Ag-110m)

Stempien et al. (2023) Table 15, printed p. 26; a different irradiation from AGR-1; the table states an estimated uncertainty of +/-10 %. UO2 compacts of capsule 3 and the Cs-134, Eu-154 and Kr-85 columns are not added. Compact 2-3-2 had one particle with a Mo-attack defect that released fission products (footnote c); it is labelled 'failed SiC' (nearest existing label) in E-406 and E-417.
